FINDINGS: the internal unit directory is framework-internal and correctly absent from encore-mock (verified live on main/corporate-pricing/develop, 3 of 21 branches); it survived PLAN_FIX_AT_SOURCE_NOT_WRAPPERS Phase 4's delete-the-dir instruction because commit 48d5933f deleted step-wrapper.spec.ts and added a replacement spec in the same commit, so the dir never emptied. CONVERTER: I machine-counted 793 bare @step() and 0 explicit labels myself rather than trust the worker — every client-facing report label is derived from a method name. Surviving design concerns, conceded by the reviewer: report text is coupled to code identifiers through the fallback label source, and check-step-labels.mjs advertises a "readable-report contract" in its header while only rejecting untranslated denied jargon — which is why "Probe edit oracle" and "Capture import all merge canaries" ship green. Root cause: all 793 were generated at runtime from method names; not one was written by a human. Fix: froze each generated string into the source as a literal @step('...'), so report output is byte-identical by construction, then deleted the converter. Proof is mechanical, not sampled — scripts/step-labels.baseline.json records what the converter produced BEFORE the change and verify-step-label-parity.mjs asserts every literal against it (793 checked, 0 unexplained differences, 21 allowed changes); it depends only on the frozen file, so it survives the converter's deletion and stays a permanent regression guard. REWORDED 21, not the 12 the council recommended: I scanned all 793 myself and found nine more nobody had flagged — five pages each rendering a step titled just "Open", BasePage.navigateTo rendering "Navigate to" with no object, and two carrying "fixture". Scope expansion was my call on objective LR-ENC-006 grounds. GATE: check-step-labels now requires a non-empty label argument (was: presence only) and strips approved client terms before scanning for denied jargon, so "Capture Location Pricing CSV rows" and "Open ECT Settings tab" pass while a bare ect/ssl still fails; approved terms are frozen in label-jargon.json because the same change deletes the jargon map they came from. Live-fired both directions and reverted the probes (diff back to 793/793). tests/_unit is finally gone — PLAN_FIX_AT_SOURCE_NOT_WRAPPERS Phase 4 called for it on 2026-07-31 but commit 48d5933f deleted one unit test and added another in the same commit, so the directory never emptied. MY ERRORS, 3: (1) my first plan draft advertised a re-runnable proof that depended on the converter it deletes — fable caught the self-contradiction; (2) two of my ticket's acceptance checks were bad instruments — a grep for "aria" matched inside "variant", and a diff-total expectation was arithmetically impossible — causing a correct worker to self-report BLOCKED; (3) my "sweep stale references" instruction sent a worker into the activity log, where it rewrote FIVE historical rows to erase mentions of the deleted files. I restored the committed history and re-appended only genuinely new rows. FABLE ruled EDIT on the plan and was right on all four claims it disputed, including a real gap I missed: scripts/check-step-labels.test.mjs imported the converter at line 9. Delegation: 7 dispatches, 2 bounces (CRLF rejoin that would have rewritten every line of 21 files; gate regex that could not parse the one label containing an apostrophe), 1 budget death. |

THE REGRESSION SEAT WAS WRONG AND I CAUGHT IT: scripts/lib/step-label-terms.mjs was untracked while origin/main:scripts/check-step-labels.mjs:34 and generate-label-inventory.mjs:16 both import it - a clean clone crashed on module-not-found the moment anyone committed, so every colleague's pre-commit was broken by my earlier push. Cause: my staging allowlist used `git add -u scripts/lib/` and -u only stages ALREADY-TRACKED files, so a brand-new file was invisible to it. The seat missed it because its orphan-import greps used "a|b" without -E, searching for a literal pipe - "No matches found" proved nothing; it also wrote that playwright --list "would pass (verified locally)" where raw output was demanded. I re-ran both correctly and git ls-tree origin/main proved the absence. I ALSO KILLED FOUR OF MY OWN FALSE FINDINGS: a crude sweep flagged walk-coverage/*.mjs as importing missing scripts/lib/ files; their imports are './lib/' relative to walk-coverage/, and walk-coverage/lib/ has all five - checked before reporting. SLOP DISPOSITION, corrected against the seat: it flagged check-step-labels.test.mjs and verify-step-label-parity.mjs as NO-READER dead code; true that nothing ran them, but twelve sibling gate-tests are each registered in package.json as test:*, so mine were UNWIRED, not dead - the /slop mode structurally cannot recommend wiring (it may only shrink surface), which is why it reached for deletion. Wired both as test:step-labels-gate + check:step-label-parity (9 tests pass; parity 793/21). Deleted the codemod (one-shot, already run, idempotent, zero readers) and, on owner's conditional go, generate-label-inventory.mjs after proving zero readers across four surfaces (npm scripts / hooks / docs / code imports). Commit a68576591 pushed to origin/main, verified 0 0. Client repo untouched by this wave - the broken gate is framework-side only, zero hits on encore-mock/main. |

| 2026-08-17T17:21 | OWNER | correction | (none) | CORRECTION to the 17:07 row above: `p68-lotA-0817` is recorded there as `ok=true`. That is FALSE. The ledger records `exit:0 ok:false exit_reason:no-deliverable death_class:C3 report_sections:0 secs:46` — the run produced nothing and its output file still carries the wrapper's NO DELIVERABLE stub. The row is left in place rather than rewritten because this log is append-only and the false entry is itself the evidence. Cause: the dispatch-row writer reports the PROCESS exit code, which the wrapper deliberately leaves at 0 so as not to mask the ledger. Any reader or tool trusting `ok=` in these dispatch rows is reading the wrong field; the ledger's `ok` / `exit_reason` are authoritative. Defect recorded for a control-surface fix in PLAN_75 §2 (B9); not fixed here because the wrapper is a protected file. |
